\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)\left(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{3B}u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}\right)=\lambda\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{3B}u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}\right)\text{ in }3B.

Then it is easy to get that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{2B}|T_{B}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{r}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{3B}\left|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{3B}u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{3B}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}|\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{3B}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+|\lambda|r\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{7/2B}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,2}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,1}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B}|G|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B}|g|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\},

where for the second inequality, we have used
(
2.22
)
with
k=n
, Poincaré’s inequality, Hölder’s inequality and for the third inequality, we have used
(
2.31
)
.

For ball
B=B(x,r)
such that it is centered at
\partial\Omega
with
0<r<\frac{1}{16}R_{0}
, write

u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}+u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,4}
in
4B\cap\Omega
, where
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}\in H_{0}^{1}(4B;\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the weak solution of
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3})-\lambda u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}=F+\operatorname{div}(f)
in
4B\cap\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}=0
on
\partial(4B\cap\Omega)
. Let

\displaystyle H_{B}=|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}|,\,\,R_{B}=|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,4}|,\,\,G_{B}=|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}|\text{ and }T_{B}=|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,4}|.

By using almost the same arguments, we can obtain
|H|\leq H_{B}+R_{B}
,
|G|\leq G_{B}+T_{B}
in
2B\cap\Omega
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{2B\cap\Omega}|R_{B}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|H|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|h|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|G_{B}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|g|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\text{ and }\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|H_{B}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|h|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, since
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,4}=u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}=0
on
\partial\Omega\cap 4B
, then in view of Poincaré’s inequality and
(
3.2
)
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{2B\cap\Omega}|T_{B}|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{r}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,4}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,4}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda,3}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|G|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{4B\cap\Omega}|g|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}.

Then by using Theorem
4.1
, we have, for any
2<p<\infty
,

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}|h|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\},

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega}|g|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right\}.

In view of Lemma
2.2
, definitions of
g,h
and Hölder’s inequality, we can complete the proof for the case
2<p<\infty
. For
1<p<2
, the results follow form duality arguments. Using the same definitions of
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}
in the proof of Lemma
2.2
, we only need to show that for any
1<p<2
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}.

For
F_{1}\in L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
f_{1}\in L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m\times d})
with
p^{\prime}=\frac{p}{p-1}
being the conjugate number of
p
, let
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}
and
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}
be solutions of Dirichlet problems:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{matrix}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)})=F_{1}&\text{in}&\Omega,\\
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}=0&\text{on}&\partial\Omega,\end{matrix}\right.\quad\text{and}\quad\left\{\begin{matrix}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)})=\operatorname{div}(f_{1})&\text{in}&\Omega,\\
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}=0&\text{on}&\partial\Omega.\end{matrix}\right.

Then it follows from direct calculations and the definition of
B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[\cdot,\cdot]
that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}F_{1}\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}}dx

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)},u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)},v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}]}=\int_{\Omega}\overline{F}v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}dx,

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}f_{1}\overline{\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}}dx

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)},u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)},v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}]}=\int_{\Omega}\overline{F}v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}dx,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}F_{1}\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}}dx

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)},u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)},v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}]}=-\int_{\Omega}\overline{f}\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}dx,

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}f_{1}\overline{\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}}dx

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)},u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)},v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}]}=-\int_{\Omega}\overline{f}\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}dx.

These, together with the results for
2<p<\infty
, imply that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}F_{1}\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-1}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|F_{1}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}f_{1}\overline{\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}F_{1}\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|F_{1}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}f_{1}\overline{\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|f\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\|f_{1}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\Omega)},

which give the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.2
.

Assume that
\Omega
is a bounded
C^{1,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
d\geq 2
and
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
. If
u_{0,\lambda}\in W^{2,p}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the unique weak solution for the Dirichlet problem

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})=F\text{ in }\Omega\quad\text{and}\quad u_{0,\lambda}=0\text{ on }\partial\Omega

with
1<p<\infty
and
F\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Then

(4.6)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)},

where
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,p,\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
. In operator forms,

(4.7)

\displaystyle\|R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{0})\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)\to W^{2,p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}.

Proof.

The results follow from the
W^{2,p}
estimates for
\mathcal{L}_{0}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C\left\{|\lambda|\|u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\right\}

and
\|u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-1}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}
, which is given by Theorem
1.2
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Assume that
\Omega
is
C^{1,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
d\geq 2
,
x_{0}\in\Omega
and
0<R<R_{0}
. If
\Delta(x_{0},2R)\neq\emptyset
, assume that
u_{0,\lambda}\in H^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},2R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the weak solution of the boundary problem

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})=0\text{ in }\Omega(x_{0},2R)\quad\text{and}\quad u_{0,\lambda}=0\text{ on }\Delta(x_{0},2R),

if
\Delta(x_{0},2R)=\emptyset
, assume that
u_{0,\lambda}\in H^{2}(B(x_{0},2R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the weak solution of the interior problem

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})=0\text{ in }B(x_{0},2R),

with
x_{0}\in\Omega
,
0<R<R_{0}
and
1<p<\infty
. Then there exists
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, a constant depending only on
d
, such that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},R)}|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{2}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},2R)}|u_{0,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

If we further assume that
\Omega
is a bounded
C^{2,1}
domain and
\rho\in(0,1)
, then

(4.9)

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{2}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},2R)}|u_{0,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\left[\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\right]_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{2+{\rho}}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},2R)}|u_{0,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
C
depends on
\mu,d,m,k,\theta_{0},\rho
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

By applying
C^{2,\rho}
estimates for the constant elliptic system (see Theorem 5.23 in
[
10
]
),
(
2.22
)
,
(
2.23
)
,
(
4.6
)
and Theorem
1.2
.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Under conditions that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
,
(
1.6
)
,
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
\Omega
is a bounded
C^{1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
d=2
, if
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
is the unique solution for the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
, then in view of
(
3.1
)
and Theorem
1.2
, we have

(4.11)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C\left\{|\lambda|\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)}+\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)}\right\}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)},

for
2<p<\infty
. If
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
is the unique solution for the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=\operatorname{div}(f)
in
\Omega
and
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
, it is not hard to see that

(4.12)

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\Omega)},\quad\text{for any }2<p<\infty,

Indeed, for any
F\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we can choose
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
such that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Then it can be easily seen by duality that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}F\overline{v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}dx

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[w_{\varepsilon,\lambda},v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]}=-\int_{\Omega}\overline{f}\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}dx

In view of Hölder’s inequality and
(
4.11
)
, it can be got that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}F\overline{v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}dx\right|\leq\|f\|_{L^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\Omega)}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)},

which directly implies
(
4.12
)
. Moreover, for
F\in\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, where
\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
denotes the dual space for the homogeneous Sobolev space
\dot{W}_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)
and
p^{\prime}
is the conjugate number of
p
, we have

(4.13)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\|F\right\|_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}.

This conclusion is also proved by using the duality arguments. Actually, for all
g\in C_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
we can choose
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
such that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=g
in
\Omega
and
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Thus,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}g\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}dx=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda},u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda},v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]}=\overline{\langle F,v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\rangle_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)\times\dot{W}_{0}^{1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}}.

By using Hölder’s inequality and
(
4.11
)
, this shows that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}g\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}dx\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\langle F,v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\rangle_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)\times\dot{W}_{0}^{1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\right|\leq\left\|F\right\|_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\left\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{\dot{W}_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|F\right\|_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\left\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\|F\right\|_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\left\|g\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)},

which gives the proof of
(
4.13
)
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
,
(
1.6
)
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
and
\Omega
is a
C^{1}
bounded domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
a=a(x)
be an atom function in
\Omega
. If
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
is the unique weak solution for the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=a
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
, then there exists a constant
C_{\theta_{0}}
depending only on
\mu,\omega(t),m,\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
such that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}.

Proof.

For atom function
a=a(\cdot)
, assume that

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}(a)\subset\Omega(x_{0},\rho)\text{ and }\left\|a\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{1}{|\Omega(x_{0},\rho)|}

with
x_{0}\in\Omega
and
0<\rho<R_{0}
. Fix
z\in\Omega
, we can choose
2<p<\infty
. Then

\displaystyle|u(z)|

\displaystyle\leq|u(z)-u_{z,\rho}|+|u_{z,\rho}|\leq C\rho^{1-\frac{2}{p}}[u]_{C^{0,1-\frac{2}{p}}(\Omega(z,\rho))}+|u_{z,\rho}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\rho^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\left\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}+\rho^{\frac{2}{p}-1}\left\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}(\Omega)}\right\},

due to Morrey’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality. Using
(
4.11
)
and
(
4.13
)
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\|a\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\rho^{\frac{2}{p}-1}\text{ and }\left\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\|a\right\|_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}.

We claim that
\left\|a\right\|_{\dot{W}^{-1,p^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq\rho^{1-\frac{2}{p}}
. It is because that for all
v\in\dot{W}_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{m})
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}a^{\alpha}(y)v^{\alpha}(y)dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{\Omega}a^{\alpha}(y)(v^{\alpha}(y)-v_{x_{0},\rho}^{\alpha})dy\right|\leq\|a\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)}\|v-v_{x_{0},\rho}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},\rho))}

\displaystyle\leq C\rho^{1-\frac{2}{p}}[v]_{C^{0,1-\frac{2}{p}}(\Omega(x_{0},\rho))}\leq C\rho^{1-\frac{2}{p}}\left\|\nabla v\right\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)},

where we have used Morrey’s theory again for the last inequality. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

The key point of
(
4.14
)
is that
C_{\theta_{0}}
does not depend on the module of
\lambda
. Such estimates are extremely important in the constructions of Green functions with
d=2
. If
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends on the module of
\lambda
, we will not be able to obtain related estimates similar to the case that
d\geq 3
.

4.2.
Green functions with dimension no less than three

Theorem 4.7

(Green functions of
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
with
d\geq 3
)
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
d\geq 3
, let
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
and
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.6
)
. Then there exists a unique Green function,
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,\cdot)):\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}
with
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq m
, such that
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)\in H^{1}(\Omega\backslash B(y,r);\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}})\cap W_{0}^{1,s}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}})
for any
s\in[1,\frac{d}{d-1})
,
y\in\Omega
and
0<r<R_{0}
.
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
satisfies

(4.15)

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),\phi(\cdot)]=\phi^{\gamma}(y),

for any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
,
\phi\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
with
p>d
. Particularly, if
F\in L^{q}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
with
q>d/2
,

(4.16)

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=\int_{\Omega}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{F(y)}dy,

satisfies the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Moreover, let
G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(x,y)
be the Green function of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I
, then

(4.17)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)=[\overline{G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(y,x)}]^{T},

which means that
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(x,y)=\overline{G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\beta\alpha}(y,x)}
for any
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq m
,
x,y\in\Omega
and
x\neq y
. For any
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in(0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the following estimates

(4.18)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-2}}\min\left\{1,\frac{[\delta(x)]^{\sigma_{1}}}{|x-y|^{\sigma_{1}}},\frac{[\delta(y)]^{\sigma_{2}}}{|x-y|^{\sigma_{2}}}\right\}

hold for any
x,y\in\Omega
and
x\neq y
, where
\delta(x)=\dist(x,\partial\Omega)
denotes the distance from
x
to the boundary of
\Omega
and
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,k,\theta_{0},\omega(t),\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

For
\lambda=0
, the results follow from constructions of Green functions of standard elliptic operators
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}
. Then we only need to consider the case that
\lambda\neq 0
. First of all, let
I(u)=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\rho)}u^{\gamma}(\cdot)
, then
I\in H^{-1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Precisely speaking, for any
u\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we can easily get by using Sobolev embedding theorem
L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega)\subset H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
that

(4.19)

\displaystyle|I(u)|\leq C|\Omega(x,\rho)|^{-\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|u\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega)}\leq C|\Omega(x,\rho)|^{-\frac{d-2}{2d}}\|u\|_{H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}\|u\|_{H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)}.

Consider the approximating Green function
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)=(G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,y))\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}})
with
\rho>0
and
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq m
, such that

(4.20)

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),u(\cdot)]=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}u^{\gamma}(\cdot)\text{ for any }u\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m}),

where
1\leq\gamma\leq m
. We see that the existence of
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
is a direct consequence of Theorem
2.1
. Choose
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\beta}(\cdot,y)

(1\leq\beta\leq m)
itself as the test function, we have

(4.21)

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\beta}(\cdot,y)]=I(G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma\beta}(\cdot,y)).

In view of
(
2.7
)
,
(
2.8
)
and
(
4.19
)
, it is not hard to see that

\displaystyle\|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}|\lambda|^{-1}\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}\|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}\|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

where
\nabla_{i}
(
i=1,2
) denote the derivatives for the first or second variable of Green functions and we will use these notations throughout this paper. Then

(4.22)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}\text{ and }\|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}|\lambda|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}.

On the other hand, using Poincaré’s inequality, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq CR_{0}\|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R_{0}\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}}.

This, combined with
(
4.22
)
and the case of
\lambda=0
, gives the following estimate

(4.23)

\displaystyle\|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\rho^{-\frac{d-2}{2}},

for any
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
and
1\leq\gamma\leq m
. Let
F\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
, consider the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. If we choose
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
as the test function, it follows from simple calculations that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\overline{G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)}F(\cdot)=B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot),G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot)]}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}\overline{u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot)}.

Suppose that
\operatorname{supp}F\subset B(y,R)\subset\Omega
, by using
(
3.6
)
, it can be got that

(4.24)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\overline{G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)}F(\cdot)\right|\leq\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(y,\rho))}\leq\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(B(y,\frac{R}{4}))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,\frac{R}{2})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{n}R^{2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,\frac{R}{2})}|F|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

for any
\rho<\frac{R}{4}
and
q>\frac{d}{2}
. In view of
(
4.24
)
, we need to estimate
\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,\frac{R}{2})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Owing to
(
2.19
)
, we can obtain without difficulty that

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}(\Omega)}\text{ and }\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}(\Omega)}.

These, together with Sobolev embedding theorem that
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\subset L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega)
, imply that

(4.25)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,\frac{R}{2})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,\frac{R}{2})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}\leq CR^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\right)^{\frac{d-2}{2d}}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|F|^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}\right)^{\frac{d+2}{2d}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\left(\int_{B(y,R)}|F|^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}\right)^{\frac{d+2}{2d}}=C_{\theta_{0}}R^{2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,R)}|F|^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}\right)^{\frac{d+2}{2d}}.

Since
d\geq 3
, we have
q>\frac{d}{2}>\frac{2d}{d+2}
. Then by using Hölder’s inequality and
(
4.25
)
, it is not hard to see that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,\frac{R}{2})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,R)}|F|^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}\right)^{\frac{d+2}{2d}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,R)}|F|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

This, together with
(
4.24
)
, gives that

(4.27)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}\overline{G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)}F(\cdot)\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{n}R^{2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,R)}|F|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

By using duality arguments, we can get that

(4.28)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y,R)}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{n}R^{2-d},

for any
\rho<\frac{R}{4}
,
R\leq\delta(x)
and
s\in(1,\frac{d}{d-2})
. For
x,y\in\Omega
such that
x\neq y
, set
r=|x-y|
. If
r\leq\frac{1}{2}\delta(y)
, choosing
p\in(1,\frac{d}{d-2})
, we have
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))=0
in
B(x,\frac{r}{2})
for any
\rho<\frac{1}{4}r
. Then by using
(
3.6
)
and
(
4.28
)
, it is easy to show that for any
s\in(1,\frac{d}{d-2})
,

(4.29)

\displaystyle|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(x,\frac{r}{2})}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}r^{2-d}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{|x-y|^{d-2}},

where we have chosen
k=n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Assume that
R<\frac{1}{4}\delta(y)
, then for any
\rho<\frac{R}{4}
, we have
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))=0
in
\Omega\backslash B(x,R)
. Choose
\varphi\in C_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R})
, such that
0\leq\varphi\leq 1
,
\varphi\equiv 1
in
\Omega\backslash B(y,2R)
,
\varphi\equiv 0
in
B(y,R)
and
|\nabla\varphi|\leq\frac{C}{R}
. Set
u(z)=\varphi^{2}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(z,y)
as the test function, then it is not hard to obtain that

(4.30)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\varphi^{2}A_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)}-\lambda\int_{\Omega}\varphi^{2}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{2}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}2\varphi A_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\nabla\varphi\overline{G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)},

where
A_{\varepsilon}(x)=A(x/\varepsilon)
if
\varepsilon>0
and
A_{0}(x)=\widehat{A}
. For
\operatorname{Re}\lambda\geq 0
, we can take the imaginary parts of both sides of
(
4.30
)
and get that

(4.31)

\displaystyle\|\varphi G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{|\lambda|}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla\varphi(\cdot)||G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)||\varphi(\cdot)||\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{|\lambda|}\left\{\|\nabla\varphi G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\delta\|\varphi\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\right\},

where
\delta>0
is sufficiently small. For
\operatorname{Re}\lambda<0
, we can take the real parts of both sides of
(
4.30
)
and obtain that
(
4.31
)
is also true. Then
(
4.31
)
is true for any
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}
. Owing to
(
4.30
)
,
(
4.31
)
and properties of
\varphi
, it is obvious that

(4.32)

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega\backslash B(y,2R))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|\nabla\varphi G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{R^{2}}\|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(B(y,2R)\backslash B(y,R))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{R^{2}}\int_{B(y,2R)\backslash B(y,R)}\frac{1}{|z-y|^{2d-4}}dz\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{2-d}.

On the other hand, if
\rho>\frac{R}{4}
, according to
(
4.22
)
, it can be obtained that

(4.33)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\backslash B(y,2R)}|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\leq\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{2-d}.

(
4.32
)
, together with
(
4.33
)
, yields that

(4.34)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\backslash B(y,2R)}|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{2-d},

for any
\rho>0
and
R<\frac{1}{4}\delta(y)
. Let
\psi\in C_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R})
such that
0\leq\psi\leq 1
,
\psi\equiv 0
in
B(y,2R)
,
\psi\equiv 1
in
\Omega\backslash B(y,\frac{5}{2}R)
and
|\nabla\psi|\leq\frac{C}{R}
. With the help of the definition of
\psi
and Sobolev embedding theorem, we can deduce that for any
\rho<\frac{R}{4}
and
R<\frac{1}{4}\delta(y)
,

(4.35)

\displaystyle\|\psi G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega)}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla(\psi G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla\psi G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}+C\|\psi\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{-d},

where for the third inequality, we have used
(
4.29
)
and
(
4.34
)
. Combining
(
4.35
)
and properties of
\psi
, it can be easily inferred that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\backslash B(y,\frac{5}{2}R)}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{-d}\text{ for any }\rho<\frac{R}{4}\text{ and }R<\frac{1}{4}\delta(y).

For the case that
\rho\geq\frac{R}{4}
, due to
(
4.22
)
, one can find that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\backslash B(y,\frac{5}{2}R)}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}\leq\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{d}{d-2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{-d}.

We now address ourselves to the uniform estimates of
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
and
\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
with respect to parameter
\rho>0
. In the case of
t>(\frac{\delta(y)}{4})^{1-d}
, we can choose
R=t^{-\frac{1}{d-1}}<\frac{1}{4}\delta(y)
and obtain that for any
\rho>0
,

(4.36)

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|>t\right\}\right|\leq CR^{d}+t^{-2}\int_{\Omega\backslash B(y,2R)}|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{d}+C_{\theta_{0}}t^{-2}R^{2-d}\leq Ct^{-\frac{d}{d-1}}+C_{\theta_{0}}t^{-2}t^{\frac{d-2}{d-1}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}t^{-\frac{d}{d-1}}.

When
t>(\frac{\delta(y)}{4})^{2-d}
, similarly, we can choose
R=t^{-\frac{1}{d-2}}
and obtain that for any
\rho>0
,

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left|\left\{x\in\Omega:|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|>t\right\}\right|\leq CR^{d}+t^{-\frac{2d}{d-2}}\int_{\Omega\backslash B(y,R)}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{d}+C_{\theta_{0}}t^{-\frac{2d}{d-2}}R^{-d}\leq Ct^{-\frac{d}{d-2}}+C_{\theta_{0}}t^{-\frac{2d}{d-2}}t^{\frac{d}{d-2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}t^{-\frac{d}{d-2}}.

Then in view of
(
4.36
)
and
(
4.37
)
, it can be shown by simple calculations that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq C[\delta(y)]^{s(2-d)}+C_{\theta_{0}}\int_{(\delta(y)/4)^{2-d}}t^{s-1}t^{-\frac{d}{d-2}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{[\delta(y)]^{s(2-d)}+[\delta(y)]^{s(2-d)+d}\right\},

for any
s\in[1,\frac{d}{d-2})
and

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{1}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)|^{s}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{[\delta(y)]^{s(1-d)}+[\delta(y)]^{s(1-d)+d}\right\},

for any
s\in[1,\frac{d}{d-1})
. From the uniform estimates above, it follows that there exists a subsequence of
\{G_{\rho_{n},\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
and
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
such that for any
s\in(1,\frac{d}{d-1})
and
1\leq\gamma\leq m
,

(4.38)

\displaystyle G_{\rho_{n},\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\rightharpoonup G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\text{ weakly in }W_{0}^{1,s}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Hence, we have, for any
\phi\in W_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
with
p>d

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),\phi(\cdot)]=\lim_{n\to\infty}B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho_{n},\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),\phi(\cdot)]=\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,{\rho_{n}})}\phi^{\gamma}(\cdot)=\phi^{\gamma}(y),

where we have used the definition of the approximating Green matrix. We note that there exists a weak solution
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
satisfying
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
for any
F\in L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
with
p>d
(
\frac{pd}{p+d}<\frac{p}{2}
). Thus we can obtain that

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(y)

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot)]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot),G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)]}=\int_{\Omega}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{F(\cdot)}.

We now verify the uniqueness. If
\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
is another Green matrix, we can also derive by representation formula that
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(y)=\int_{\Omega}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{F(\cdot)}
. It follows from the uniqueness of the weak solution that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}[\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)]\overline{F(\cdot)}=0\text{ for any }F\in L^{\frac{p}{2}}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Then
\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)=G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
a.e. in
\Omega
due to the arbitrariness of
F
. Next, let
G_{\tau,\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(\cdot,x)
denote the approximating for Green functions of the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I
, which satisfy

(4.39)

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[G_{\tau,\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\xi}(\cdot,x),u(\cdot)]=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\tau)}u^{\xi}(\cdot)\text{ for any }u\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m}).

By the same argument, we can derive the existence and uniqueness of
G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\xi}(\cdot,x)
. Thus for any
\tau,\rho>0
and
1\leq\gamma,\xi\leq m
, it is obvious to see that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}G_{\tau,\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\gamma\xi}(\cdot,x)

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\xi}(\cdot,x)]

\displaystyle=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\xi}(\cdot,x),G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)]}=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\tau)}\overline{G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\xi\gamma}(\cdot,y)}.

Note that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(G_{\tau,\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\xi}(\cdot,x))=0
in
\Omega\backslash B(x,\tau)
and
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))=0
in
\Omega\backslash B(y,\rho)
. In view of the
W^{1,p}
estimates,
G_{\tau,\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\xi}(\cdot,x)
and
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)
are locally Hölder continuous. Therefore, by taking
\tau,\rho\to\infty
, we have
\overline{G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\xi\gamma}(x,y)}=G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}^{\gamma\xi}(y,x)
, which implies that
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)=[\overline{G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(y,x)}]^{T}
for any
x,y\in\Omega
such that
x\neq y
. This gives the proof of
(
4.17
)
.

Finally, we will prove
(
4.18
)
. Let
r=|x-y|
and
F\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega(x,{\frac{r}{3}});\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Assume that
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
is the solution of
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Then
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y)=\int_{\Omega}\overline{F(\cdot)}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)
. Since
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega\backslash\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3})
and
\Omega(y,\frac{r}{3})\subset\Omega\backslash\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3})
, it follows from
(
2.19
)
and
(
3.6
)
that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\frac{r}{3})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega(y,\frac{r}{3}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}(\Omega)}=\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{1-\frac{d}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d+2}}(\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{2-\frac{d}{2}}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3}))}.

In view of duality arguments, we can infer that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(4.40)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}r^{2-d}.

Note that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))=0
in
\Omega\backslash B(y,r)
for any
r>0
. So in the case of
\frac{r}{6}\leq\delta(x)
, it follows from
(
3.6
)
and
(
4.40
)
that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{d-2}}.

When
\frac{r}{6}>\delta(x)
, on the other hand, in view of
(
3.5
)
and
(
4.40
)
, for any
\sigma_{1}\in(0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\overline{x},y)|\leq[G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)]_{C^{0,\sigma_{1}}(\Omega(x,\frac{r}{6}))}|x-\overline{x}|^{\sigma_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\frac{|x-\overline{x}|}{r}\right)^{\sigma_{1}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{r}{3})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}}\frac{[\delta(x)]^{\sigma_{1}}}{|x-y|^{d-2+\sigma_{1}}},

where
\overline{x}\in\partial\Omega
is the point such that
|x-\overline{x}|=\delta(x)
. It is easy to complete the proof of
(
4.18
)
by considering the same estimates for
G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(x,y)
and using property
(
4.17
)
.
∎

4.3.
Two dimensional Green functions

Theorem 4.8

(Green functions of
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
with
d=2
)
.

Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
,
(
1.6
)
,
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
\Omega
is a bounded
C^{1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. If
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, then there exists a unique Green function
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,\cdot)):\Omega\times\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}\cup\left\{\infty\right\}
with
1\leq\alpha,\beta\leq m
, such that

(4.41)

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)\in\operatorname{BMO}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}})\text{ i.e. }\left\|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)\right\|_{\BMO(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\text{ uniformly for }y\in\Omega.

Moreover, for all
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
being the weak solution for the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
, where
F\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
,
(p>1)
, we have

(4.42)

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=\int_{\Omega}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{F(y)}dy.

Furthermore, for Green functions
G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(x,y)
corresponding to the operators of
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I
, we have
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)=\overline{[G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(y,x)]}^{T}
. For all
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma\in(0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, Green functions satisfy pointwise estimates

(4.43)

|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x-y|}\right)^{\sigma}\text{ for any }x,y\in\Omega,

(4.44)

|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq C_{k,\theta_{0}}\left\{1+|\lambda|^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}R_{0}^{\sigma}+\ln\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x-y|}\right)\right\}\text{ for any }x,y\in\Omega,

(4.45)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}[\delta(x)]^{\sigma_{1}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{\sigma_{1}}}

\displaystyle\text{ if }\delta(x)<\frac{1}{4}|x-y|,

(4.46)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}[\delta(y)]^{\sigma_{2}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{\sigma_{2}}}

\displaystyle\text{ if }\delta(y)<\frac{1}{4}|x-y|,

(4.47)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}[\delta(x)]^{\sigma_{1}}[\delta(y)]^{\sigma_{2}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}}

\displaystyle\text{ if }\min\left\{\delta(x),\delta(y)\right\}<\frac{1}{4}|x-y|,

where
\delta(x)=\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)
denotes the distance from
x
to the boundary of
\Omega
,
x\neq y
and
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
are constants depending only on
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma,\mu,\omega(t),k,\theta_{0},m
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

For any
y\in\Omega
and
\rho>0
, in view of Theorem
2.1
, there exists a matrix-valued function
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)=(G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,y)):\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}}
, such that for any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
,
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
satisfies

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),u(\cdot)]=\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}u^{\gamma}(\cdot)\text{ for any }u\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m}).

For any atom function in
\Omega
denoted by
a=a(\cdot)
, s.t.

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}(a)\subset\Omega(y,\rho)\text{ and }\left\|a\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{1}{|\Omega(y,\rho)|},

we can get
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{m})
such that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=a
in
\Omega
and
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Then

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot)]=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot),G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)]}=\int_{\Omega}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{a^{\alpha}(\cdot)}.

This, together with
(
4.14
)
, implies the following estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)\overline{a(\cdot)}\right|\leq\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot)\right|\leq\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(y,\rho))}\leq\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}},

where
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,\omega(t),\theta_{0},m
and
\Omega
. According to the fact that
\mathcal{H}^{1}
is the dual space of
\operatorname{BMO}
space, we can derive that
G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)
has a uniform boundedness
C_{\theta_{0}}
in
\operatorname{BMO}
space, where
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,\omega(t),m,\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
. In view of Banach-Alaoglu theorem, we have, for all
y\in\Omega
, there exists a sequence
\{\rho_{j}\}
such that
\rho_{j}\to 0
when
j\to\infty
and functions
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,y)\in\operatorname{BMO}(\Omega)
such that
G_{\rho_{j},\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,y)
converge to
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(\cdot,y)
in the space
\operatorname{BMO}(\Omega)
with the sense of the weak*-topology. For
F\in L^{q}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{m})
where
q>1
, we can choose
1<q_{1}<q
,
p=\frac{2q_{1}}{2-q_{1}}>2
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in W_{0}^{1,p}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{m})
, such that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Then it can be obtained that

(4.48)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho)}u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y),u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot)]

\displaystyle=\overline{B_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda},\Omega}[u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot),G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y)]}=\int_{\Omega}G_{\rho,\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{F^{\alpha}(\cdot)}.

In view of Remark
4.4
, Poincaré’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\right\|_{W^{1,p}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\|F\right\|_{L^{q_{1}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\|F\right\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)},

where
C_{\theta_{0}}
is a constant depending only on
\mu,\omega(t),\theta_{0},m,p,q
and
\Omega
. Using Sobolev embedding theorem, we see that
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
is continuous. Letting
\rho_{j}\to 0
, the left hand side of
(
4.48
)
converges to
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y)
. On the other hand, according to the embedding theorem
L^{p}\subset\mathcal{H}^{1}
, one can obtain that

(4.49)

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(y)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y,\rho_{j})}u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\int_{\Omega}G_{\rho_{j},\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{F^{\alpha}(\cdot)}=\int_{\Omega}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\gamma}(\cdot,y)\overline{F^{\alpha}(\cdot)}.

Since the representation formula, uniqueness of Green functions and duality property follow from almost the same arguments for the case
d\geq 3
, here we will not repeat the proofs of them.

Finally, we will prove the pointwise estimates of Green functions. Namely, we will prove
(
4.43
)
-
(
4.47
)
. Letting
x_{0},y_{0}\in\Omega
with
x_{0}\neq y_{0}
and assuming that
\delta(x_{0})<\frac{1}{2}|x_{0}-y_{0}|=\frac{1}{2}r
, we have
\Omega(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}r)\subset\Omega\backslash\left\{y_{0}\right\}
. According to the definition of
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0})
, we have
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0}))=0
in
\Omega(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}r)
and
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0})=0
on
\partial\Omega\cap B(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}r)
. Then by
(
3.6
)
, we can obtain that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|\leq C\left\|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{1}{2}r)}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|.

According to the definition of
\BMO(\Omega)
and the observation that
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},\frac{r}{2}}=0
by
(
2.6
)
, it is easy to see that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{2})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(z,y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(z,y_{0})_{x_{0},\frac{r}{2}}|dz

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}\left\|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{\BMO(\Omega)}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}},

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,\omega(t),k,\theta_{0},m
and
\Omega
. With the help of estimates above, we can find that if
\delta(x)<\frac{1}{2}|x-y|
, then

(4.50)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}}.

Assume that
\delta(x_{0})<\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|=\frac{1}{4}r
and
z_{0}\in\partial\Omega
is chosen such that
|x_{0}-z_{0}|=\delta(x_{0})
, we note that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0}))=0
in
\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{2})
and
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0})=0
on
\partial\Omega\cap B(z_{0},\frac{r}{2})
. In view of the localized boundary Hölder estimates
(
3.5
)
, then for all
\sigma_{1}\in(0,1)
, there is

(4.51)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle=|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x_{0},y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(z_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq|x_{0}-z_{0}|^{\sigma_{1}}[G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0})]_{C^{0,\sigma_{1}}(\Omega(z_{0},\frac{3r}{8}))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\frac{[\delta(x_{0})]}{r}\right)^{\sigma_{1}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(z_{0},\frac{r}{2})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Simple obervations give that for all
x\in\Omega(z_{0},\frac{r}{2})
, we have
\delta(x)<\frac{1}{2}r
. Then due to
(
4.50
)
and
(
4.51
)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}[\delta(x_{0})]^{\sigma_{1}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{\sigma_{1}}}.

This proves
(
4.45
)
. On the other hand,
(
4.46
)
can be obtained naturally by considering the same estimates for Green functions of
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I
. In addition, we can see from the above proof that
\frac{1}{4}
is not an essential constant in
(
4.45
)
-
(
4.46
)
. That is,
(
4.45
)
-
(
4.46
)
are also true if we change
\frac{1}{4}
to any constants
C_{0}
such that
0<C_{0}<\frac{1}{2}
by using almost the same arguments. Next, let us prove
(
4.47
)
. We can assume that
\delta(x_{0})<\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
and
\delta(y_{0})<\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
. Otherwise,
(
4.47
)
follows from
(
4.45
)
-
(
4.46
)
directly. By using almost the same methods, we have, for any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
,

(4.52)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\frac{[\delta(x_{0})]}{r}\right)^{\sigma_{1}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(z_{0},\frac{7r}{16})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For all
y\in B(z_{0},\frac{7r}{16})\cap\Omega
, it is esay to find by triangular inequality that
|y-y_{0}|\geq|x_{0}-y_{0}|-|x_{0}-y|\geq\frac{9}{16}r
. Meanwhile, it is not hard to obtain that

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(z,y_{0})|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}[\delta(y_{0})]^{\sigma_{2}}}{(1+|\lambda||z-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|z-y_{0}|^{\sigma_{2}}}\text{ for any }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\text{ and }z\in\Omega\left(z_{0},\frac{7r}{16}\right).

This, together with
(
4.52
)
, gives the proof of
(
4.47
)
. Indeed, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\theta_{0}}\left(\frac{[\delta(x_{0})]}{r}\right)^{\sigma_{1}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(z_{0},\frac{7r}{16})\cap\Omega}\frac{[\delta(y_{0})]^{2\sigma_{2}}}{(1+|\lambda||y-y_{0}|^{2})^{2k}|y-y_{0}|^{2\sigma_{2}}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}[\delta(x_{0})]^{\sigma_{1}}[\delta(y_{0})]^{\sigma_{2}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}}.

At last, if
\delta(x_{0})\geq\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
and
\delta(y_{0})\geq\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
, we choose
F\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4});\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Obviously, here,
\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})=B(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})
. Let
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
satisfy
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. In view of the representation theorem, we have that
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y)=\int_{\Omega}\overline{G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)}F(\cdot)
. Since
F\equiv 0
in
\Omega\backslash\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})
, then
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega\backslash\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})
. For all
p>2
and
1<q<2
, by using
(
3.6
)
and Sobolev embedding theorem
H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)\subset L^{p}(\Omega)
with
p>1
, it is not hard to get that

\displaystyle|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=C_{\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{\frac{2}{p}}r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{\frac{2}{p}+2-\frac{2}{q}}r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|F|^{q}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{\frac{2}{p}+2-\frac{2}{q}}r^{-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{2}{q}-1}\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|F|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where for the third inequality, we have used
(
2.20
)
. This implies that

(4.53)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\overline{F(\cdot)}\right|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}r\left(\frac{R_{0}}{r}\right)^{\frac{2}{p}-\frac{2}{q}+2}\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|F|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Owing to duality arguments,
(
4.53
)
implies that for all
p>2
and
1<q<2

(4.54)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\frac{R_{0}}{r}\right)^{\frac{2}{p}-\frac{2}{q}+2}.

For any
\sigma\in(0,1)
, we can choose special
p,q
such that
-\frac{2}{p}+\frac{2}{q}-2=-\sigma
. Then
(
4.54
)
, together with
(
3.6
)
, gives the proof of
(
4.43
)
. Of course, there is still a certain distance between this and
(
4.44
)
, so we need to make more precise estimates.

For
x_{0},y_{0}\in\Omega
, letting
r_{1}=\frac{1}{2}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
, it can be seen that if
\delta(x_{0})<\frac{1}{2}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
, then by
(
4.50
)
, we have
|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}
. If
\delta(x_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}|x_{0}-y_{0}|
, we need to consider the sequence of subsets of
\Omega
denoted as
\Omega_{j}=\Omega(x_{0},2^{j}r_{1})
with
j=0,1,...,N
such that
2^{N}r_{1}\geq\delta(x_{0})
and
2^{N-1}r_{1}<\delta(x_{0})
. Obviously, we can bound
N
by the inequality

(4.55)

\displaystyle N\leq C\left\{1+\ln\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}\right)\right\}.

According to the fact that
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\in\operatorname{BMO}(\Omega)
, we have, if
1\leq j\leq N-2
, then

(4.56)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},2^{j}r_{1}}-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},2^{j+1}r_{1}}|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},2^{j}r_{1})}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},2^{j+1}r_{1})}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},2^{j+1}r_{1})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},2^{j+1}r_{1}}|dx

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{\BMO(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}.

With the choice of
N
, we can get that

(4.57)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{N-1}}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{N}}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|\leq C\left\|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{\BMO(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}.

In the view of
(
3.6
)
, if
p>2
and
q=\frac{2p}{p+2}
, then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r_{1}^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},r_{1})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|dx

(4.58)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{k,\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r_{1}^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r_{1}^{2}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r_{1}^{2})^{k}}+C_{k,\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r_{1}^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r_{1}^{2}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|,

where the second inequality in
(
4.58
)
is derived from
(
4.56
)
and the fact that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},r_{1})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|dx\leq\left\|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right\|_{\BMO(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}.

In view of
(
4.56
)
,
(
4.57
)
and
(
4.58
)
, it is not hard to see that

(4.59)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=-1}^{N-2}\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{j}}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})-\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{j+1}}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right|+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{N-1}}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(1+\ln\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}\right)\right)+C|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(1+\ln\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}\right)\right)+C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r_{1}^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r_{1}^{2}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|,

where we denote
\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{-1}}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})=G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})
and
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,\omega(t),\theta_{0},m
and
\Omega
. Setting
\sigma\in(0,1)
and using
(
4.43
)
, then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{0}}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\right|\leq\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{0}}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(x_{0},r_{1})}\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{\sigma}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x-y_{0}|^{\sigma}}dx\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{\sigma}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{\sigma}}.

Choosing
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that
k>n
, we have

\displaystyle C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r_{1}^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r_{1}^{2}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})_{x_{0},r_{1}}|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}|\lambda|^{\frac{\sigma}{2}}R_{0}^{\sigma}.

This, together with
(
4.59
)
, completes the proof.
∎

4.4.
More estimates of Green functions

Theorem 4.9
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1,\eta}

(0<\eta<1)
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
