and
(
1.5
)
. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the Green functions of
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)
satisfy the uniform pointwise estimates

(4.60)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|+|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-1}},

(4.61)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(y)}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}},

(4.62)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(x)}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}},

(4.63)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}},

for
x,y\in\Omega
and
x\neq y
, where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\tau,\nu,k,\theta_{0},\eta
and
\Omega
.

Remark 4.10
.

The Lipschitz estimates of Green functions for operators
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
given above are sharp. These are of great significance in this paper. Further proofs rely heavily on these estimates. In fact, we point out that after proving these estimates, the proofs of theorems on convergence rates for Green functions are standard and the reason why the standard proofs can be carried out later is that we calculated the impact of
\lambda
in Lipschitz estimates to the best.

Remark 4.11
.

The proof of Theorem
4.9
is quite different from which of Lipschitz estimates of Green functions for the operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}
, especially when
d=2
. Recall that in
[
21
]
, the case
d=2
was discussed by using the fact that
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}(c)=0
for any constant vector
c\in\mathbb{C}^{m}
. However operator
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
do not have such property. In this point of view, we need to modify the proofs in
[
21
]
and use some technical calculations here.

Proof of Theorem
4.9
.

Firstly, we consider the case
d\geq 3
. Under the condition that
A
is Hölder continuous, we can improve the estimates
(
4.18
)
to the case that
\sigma_{1}=\sigma_{2}=1
. Setting
R=|x-y|
and applying
(
4.18
)
, it is apparent that if
\frac{R}{6}\leq\delta(x)
, then

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{d-2}},

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. If
\frac{R}{6}>\delta(x)
, we can choose
\overline{x}\in\partial\Omega
such that
|x-\overline{x}|=\delta(x)
. Then it follows from
(
3.8
)
that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\overline{x},y)|\leq|x-\overline{x}|\|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(x,\frac{R}{6}))}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(x)}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{R}{3})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This, together with
(
4.40
)
, implies that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(x)}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-1}}.

By applying the same arguments on
G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(x,y)
and using
(
4.17
)
, we have

(4.64)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-2}}\min\left\{1,\frac{\delta(x)}{|x-y|},\frac{\delta(y)}{|x-y|}\right\}.

Noticing
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))=0
for any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
in
\Omega\backslash\{y\}
, it follows from
(
3.8
)
and
(
4.40
)
that

(4.65)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{R}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{1}{2}R)}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-1}}.

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Again, in view of
(
4.17
)
, we can see that
(
4.60
)
is true. Similarly, if we use the estimate

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(y)}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-1}}

for the second inequality of
(
4.65
)
, it is easy to show that

(4.66)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(y)}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}},

which gives the proof of
(
4.61
)
. Similarly,
(
4.62
)
can be derived by using
(
4.17
)
and
(
4.66
)
. Now, we only need to show
(
4.63
)
. Obviously, for any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
,

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(\cdot,y))=0\quad\text{in}\quad\Omega\backslash\{y\}.

For any
k_{1},k_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
, using
(
4.60
)
, we have

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k_{2}}R}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{1}{2}R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k_{2}}R}\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k_{1}}R^{d-1}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}},

which completes the proof for the case
d\geq 3
. Next, we consider the case
d=2
. Set
r=|x_{0}-y_{0}|
with
x_{0}\neq y_{0}
and
x_{0},y_{0}\in\Omega
. For
F\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4});\mathbb{C}^{m})
, let
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
satisfy
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. Then
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y)=\int_{\Omega}\overline{G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y)}F(\cdot)
due to representation formula
(
4.42
)
. Since
F\equiv 0
in
\Omega\backslash\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})
, we have,
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega\backslash\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})
. Owing to
(
3.6
)
,
(
4.11
)
, Poincaré’s inequality and Hölder’s inequality, we can get, for any
p>2
,

\displaystyle|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}=C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}.

By duality arguments, this implies that

(4.67)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})}{r}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})}{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}.

According to the Lipschitz estimates
(
3.8
)
and
(
4.67
)
, then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k}r}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{0})}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2}}.

Then
(
4.61
)
is proved and
(
4.62
)
follows directly by considering same estimates of
G_{\varepsilon,\overline{\lambda}}(x,y)
. Moreover, for
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
, we have
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})_{y_{0},\frac{r}{4}})=\overline{\lambda}(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})_{y_{0},\frac{r}{4}}
in
\Omega
. By using
(
2.10
)
,
(
3.8
)
,
(
4.11
)
, Hölder’s inequality and Poincaré’s inequality, it can be obtained that

\displaystyle|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{r}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})_{y_{0},\frac{r}{4}}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r|(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})_{y_{0},\frac{r}{4}}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}|\lambda|r\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=C_{\theta_{0}}r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\left(\int_{\Omega}|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}r^{-\frac{2}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}+C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))},

when
2<p<\infty
. In view of the definition of
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
, we can get that

(4.68)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})\overline{F(\cdot)}\right|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4}))}.

Again, by duality arguments, it can be inferred that

(4.69)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(\cdot,y_{0})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}}{r}.

Similarly, with the help of Lipschitz estimates
(
3.8
)
and
(
4.69
)
, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k+n}r}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},\frac{r}{4})}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(z,y_{0})|^{2}dz\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{n}}{(1+|\lambda|r^{2})^{k+n}r^{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2}}.

This completes the proof of
(
4.63
)
. Then we only need to show
(
4.60
)
. To begin with, we note that if
|y-z|<\frac{1}{2}|x-y|
, then

(4.70)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,z))|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(y,\frac{|x-y|}{2}))}|y-z|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}|y-z|}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|},

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. If
\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|\leq\delta(y_{0})
, we get that

(4.71)

\displaystyle|x-x_{0}|\leq\frac{1}{2}|x-y_{0}|\text{ for any }x\in B\left(x_{0},\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|\right).

At first, there exists a point
\overline{y}\in\partial\Omega
(see Figure 1), such that

\displaystyle(x_{0}-\overline{y})//(x_{0}-y_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad(x_{0}-\overline{y})\cdot(x_{0}-y_{0})>0.

Then according to the fact that
\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|\leq\delta(y_{0})\leq|y_{0}-\overline{y}|
, there always exists a positive integer
N\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and a sequence of points
\left\{y_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{N}\subset\Omega
such that

\displaystyle y_{j}=x_{0}+\frac{5}{4}(y_{j-1}-x_{0}),\ j=1,\ldots,N,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{N-1}|\leq|y_{N-1}-\overline{y}|\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{N}|>|y_{N}-\overline{y}|.

Moreover, in view of the definition of
\left\{y_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{N}
, one can obtain that

(4.72)

|x_{0}-y_{j}|=\frac{5}{4}|x_{0}-y_{j-1}|=\ldots=\left(\frac{5}{4}\right)^{j}|x_{0}-y_{0}|,\ \ j=1,\ldots,N.

x_{0}
y_{0}
y_{1}
y_{2}
y_{3}
y_{N}
\overline{y}
\partial\Omega

Figure 1.

By applying
(
4.70
)
, we can obtain that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(4.73)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{1}))|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|}.

Similarly, since
|y_{j+1}-y_{j}|=\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{j}|<\frac{1}{2}|x_{0}-y_{j}|
, we have

(4.74)

|\nabla_{1}(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{j})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{j+1}))|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{j}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{j}|},

for any
j=1,\ldots,N-1
. Finally, owing to the simple observation that

\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{N}|>|y_{N}-\overline{y}|\geq\delta(y_{N}),

it can be inferred that

(4.75)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{N})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(y_{N})}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{N}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{N}|^{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{N}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{N}|},

where we have used
(
4.61
)
. From (
4.73
)-(
4.75
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N}\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{j}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{j}|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N}\left(\frac{4}{5}\right)^{j}\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|\left(\frac{5}{4}\right)^{2j}|x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}|x_{0}-y_{0}|},

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\frac{1}{4}|x_{0}-y_{0}|\leq\delta(y_{0})
. This, together with
(
4.61
)
and
(
4.17
)
, gives
(
4.60
)
.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

Under the same assumptions of Theorem
4.8
, if we further assume that
A
satisfies
(
1.5
)
, we can improve
(
4.44
)
to the sharp estimate. That is, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(4.76)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}}\left\{1+\ln\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x-y|}\right)\right\},

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,m,\tau,\nu,k,\theta_{0},\eta
and
\Omega
. The proof is similar to Theorem
4.9
. Like what we have done in
(
4.70
)
, one can find that if
|y-z|<\frac{1}{2}|x-y|
, then

(4.77)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,z)|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(y,\frac{|x-y|}{2}))}|y-z|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}|y-z|}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}},

where we have used
(
4.60
)
. Then using almost the same arguments, we can obtain that

(4.78)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{j})-G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{j+1})|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}},

for any
j=0,1,2,\ldots,N-1
. Also, it can be obtained that

(4.79)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{N})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{N}|^{2})^{k}},

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, where we have used
(
4.50
)
. Noticing that the boundedness of
N
,
(
4.55
)
, is also true in this case, it follows from direct computations that

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N}\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{j}|^{2})^{k}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x_{0}-y_{0}|^{2})^{k}}\left\{1+\ln\left(\frac{R_{0}}{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}\right)\right\},

which completes the proof.

Lemma 4.13
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
d\geq 2
, let
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
and
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.6
)
. Then for any
x\in\Omega
,
\sigma\in(0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(4.80)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-1}\cdot\left\{\begin{matrix}1&\text{if}&d\geq 3,\\
(1+|\lambda|R_{0}^{2})^{\sigma}&\text{if}&d=2,\end{matrix}\right.

(4.81)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\sigma,\omega(t),\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
. If we further assume that
A
satisfies
(
1.5
)
and
\Omega
is a
C^{1,\eta}
domain with
0<\eta<1
, then

(4.82)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where
C
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\tau,\nu,\theta_{0},\eta
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

By using
(
4.18
)
with
k=2
, it is easy to infer that

(4.83)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x,R_{0})}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{2}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{d-2}}dy=\int_{0}^{R_{0}}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}\rho}{(1+|\lambda|\rho^{2})^{2}}d\rho

\displaystyle=-\left.\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{|\lambda|(1+|\lambda|\rho^{2})}\right|_{0}^{R_{0}}=C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\frac{1}{|\lambda|}-\frac{1}{|\lambda|(1+|\lambda|R_{0}^{2})}\right\}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{|\lambda|}.

On the other hand, it can be easily seen that,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B(x,R_{0})}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{2}}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{d-2}}dy\leq\int_{0}^{R_{0}}C_{\theta_{0}}\rho d\rho\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{2}.

This, together with
(
4.83
)
, implies
(
4.80
)
with
d\geq 3
. For
d=2
, owing to
(
4.43
)
, we can get that, for any
\sigma\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{\Omega(x,2^{-j}R_{0})\backslash\Omega(x,2^{-j-1}R_{0})}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}R_{0}^{\sigma}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{2}|x-y|^{\sigma}}dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}(2^{-j}R_{0})^{2}R_{0}^{\sigma}}{(1+|\lambda|(2^{-j}R_{0})^{2})^{1-\sigma}(2^{-j}R_{0})^{\sigma}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(1+|\lambda|R_{0}^{2})^{\sigma}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-1}.

For
R>0
, consider the annulus
\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)
. One can use small
d
dimensional balls, whose radius are
\frac{5}{8}R
and centers are on
\partial B(x,\frac{3}{2}R)\cap\Omega
, to cover the annulus
\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)
. We denote these small balls as
\left\{B(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)\right\}_{i=1}^{N}
. Obviously,
N\leq C\frac{\pi((2R)^{d}-R^{d})}{\pi(\frac{5}{8}R)^{d}}\leq C
, where
C
is a constant, independent of
R
. Then by using Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R^{\frac{d}{2}}.

In view of Caccioppoli’s inequality
(
2.23
)
and
(
4.18
)
, if
d\geq 3
, then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{i},\frac{2}{3}R)}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R^{\frac{d}{2}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{d-1}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{\frac{d}{2}-1}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}.

Thus

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\int_{\Omega(x,2^{-j}R_{0})\backslash\Omega(x,2^{-j-1}R_{0})}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}(2^{-j}R_{0})}{(1+|\lambda|(2^{-j}R_{0})^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}

\displaystyle\approx\int_{B(0,2R_{0})}\frac{1}{(1+|\lambda||x|^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\frac{1}{|x|^{d-1}}dy\leq C(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

which gives the proof of
(
4.81
)
with
d\geq 3
. If
d=2
, to show
(
4.81
)
, we have

(4.84)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\int_{\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}R.

Choose
f\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R);\mathbb{C}^{m\times d})
,
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
such that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=\operatorname{div}(f)
in
\Omega
and
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
in
\partial\Omega
. Then
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(z)=\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(z,\cdot)\overline{f(\cdot)}
due to
(
4.42
)
. In view of
(
3.6
)
,
(
4.12
)
, Hölder’s inequality, the fact that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega\backslash\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)
and
\Omega(x,\frac{1}{20}R)\subset\Omega\backslash\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)
, it is not hard to deduce that for any
2<p<\infty
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x,\frac{1}{20}R)}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p-2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R^{-1+\frac{2}{p}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\|f\|_{L^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\Omega)}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R))}.

Then by using the representation of
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
given above, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)}\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{f(y)}dy\right|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R))}.

By duality arguments, we can infer that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\Omega(x_{i},\frac{5}{8}R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}.

With the help of
(
4.84
)
, it can be seen that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(4.85)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega(x,2R)\backslash\Omega(x,R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}R}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}.

Then according to the calculations with
d\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\int_{\Omega(x,2^{-j}R_{0})\backslash\Omega(x,2^{-j-1}R_{0})}|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}2^{-j}R_{0}}{(1+|\lambda|(2^{-j}R_{0})^{2})^{\frac{3}{2}}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

For the proof of
(
4.82
)
, we simply note that by
(
4.60
)
with
k=2
,

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{2}|x-y|^{d-1}}dy\leq C_{\theta_{0}}(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Then we can complete the proof.
∎

5.
Convergence estimates of resolvents

5.1.
Convergence of Green functions

Now we turn to estimate
|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)-G_{0,\lambda}(x,y)|
, where
G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
and
G_{0,\lambda}
are Green functions for operators
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
and
\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I
respectively. Such estimates are essential in the proof of Theorem
1.3
and
1.4
.

Theorem 5.1

(Convergence of Green functions I)
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
,
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.5
)
. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
x,y\in\Omega
with
x\neq y
,

(5.1)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)-G_{0,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-1}},

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\nu,\tau,k,\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
.

For continuous function
u
in
\Omega
, the nontangential maximal function is defined by

(5.2)

\displaystyle(u)^{*}(y)=\sup\left\{|u(x)|:x\in\Omega\text{ and }|x-y|<C_{0}\delta(x)\right\}

for
y\in\partial\Omega
, where
C_{0}=C_{0}(\Omega)>1
is sufficiently large depending on
\Omega
.

Theorem 5.2

(Nontangential-maximal-function estimates)
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1,\eta}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
0<\eta<1
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
,
(
1.5
)
and
1<p\leq\infty
. For
g\in L^{p}(\partial\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
, let
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
be the unique solution to the Dirichlet problem
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=g
on
\partial\Omega
with the property
(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})^{*}\in L^{p}(\partial\Omega)
. Then

(5.3)

\displaystyle\|(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})^{*}\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|g\|_{L^{p}(\partial\Omega)}.

where
C_{\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\nu,\tau,\theta_{0},\eta
and
\Omega
. In the case that
p=\infty
,
(
5.3
)
implies,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}.

Proof.

By using the definition of Green functions, we can represent
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
by the formula

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=\int_{\partial\Omega}P_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{g(y)}dS(y),

where the Poisson kernel
P_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)=(P_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(x,y))
for
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
in
\Omega
is given by

\displaystyle P_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(x,y)=-n_{i}(y)a_{ji}^{\gamma\beta}(y/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{j}}\{G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\gamma}(x,y)\}

for
x\in\Omega
,
y\in\partial\Omega
and
n(x)=(n_{1}(x),n_{2}(x),...,n_{d}(x))
denotes the outward unit normal to
\partial\Omega
. In view of the estimate
(
4.62
)
, we have, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle|P_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\delta(x)}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(x)}{|x-y|^{d}}.

Hence, we can obtain that

\displaystyle|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)|\leq\int_{\partial\Omega}\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}\delta(x)}{|x-y|^{d}}|g(y)|dS(y)\text{ for any }x\in\Omega.

The rest of the proof is standard, which can be obtained in Theorem 4.6.5 of
[
21
]
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
,
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.5
)
. If
\Delta(x_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
, assume that
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H^{1}(\Omega(x_{0},3R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
is a solution of the boundary problem

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0\text{ in }\Omega(x_{0},3R)\quad\text{and}\quad u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=f\text{ on }\Delta(x_{0},3R),

with
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}<\infty
. If
\Delta(x_{0},3R)=\emptyset
, assume that
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H^{1}(B(x_{0},3R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the weak solution of the interior problem

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0\text{ in }B(x_{0},3R).

Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}\leq C_{k,\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Delta(x_{0},3R))}+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(x_{0},{3R})}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|,

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\theta_{0},k,\nu,\tau
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

We only need to show the case that
\Delta(x_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
since the other follows directly from
(
3.6
)
. If
f\equiv 0
, the estimate is also a consequence of
(
3.6
)
. To treat the general case, let
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
be the solution to
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
with the Dirichlet condition
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=f
on
\partial\widetilde{\Omega}\cap\partial\Omega
and
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\widetilde{\Omega}\backslash\partial\Omega
, where
\widetilde{\Omega}
is a
C^{1,1}
domain such that
\Omega(x_{0},2R)\subset\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\Omega(x_{0},3R)
. By the maximum principle
(
5.4
)
, we have,

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{\Omega})}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\widetilde{\Omega})}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Delta(x_{0},3R))}.

This, together with the fact that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{2R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Delta_{3R})},

gives the results.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

For
\varepsilon\geq 0
and
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{1,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.5
)
. Let
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H^{1}(\Omega(x_{0},4R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
u_{0,\lambda}\in W^{2,p}(\Omega(x_{0},4R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
for some
d<p<\infty
. If
\Delta(x_{0},4R)\neq\emptyset
, assume that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})\text{ in }\Omega(x_{0},4R)\quad\text{and}\quad u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=u_{0,\lambda}\text{ on }\Delta(x_{0},4R),

and if
\Delta(x_{0},4R)=\emptyset
, assume that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})\text{ in }B(x_{0},4R).

Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{4R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|

\displaystyle+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\left\{R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega_{4R})}+(1+|\lambda|R^{2})\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\},

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends on
\mu,d,m,\nu,\tau,p,k,\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

Firstly, we consider the case that
\Delta(x_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
. Choose a domain
\widetilde{\Omega}
, which is
C^{1,1}
such that
\Omega(x_{0},3R)\subset\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\Omega(x_{0},4R)
. Consider

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)-u_{0,\lambda}(x)-\varepsilon\chi_{j}^{\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}(x)+w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}(x)\quad\text{in }\widetilde{\Omega},

where
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}
and
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}
are weak solutions for the Drichlet problems

(5.7)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})\text{ in }\widetilde{\Omega}\quad\text{and}\quad w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\in H_{0}^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega};\mathbb{C}^{m}),

(5.8)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=0\text{ in }\widetilde{\Omega}\quad\text{and}\quad w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\quad\text{on }\partial\widetilde{\Omega}.

Since
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=-\varepsilon\chi(x/\varepsilon)\nabla u_{0,\lambda}
on
\Delta(x_{0},3R)
and
\|\chi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C
, it follows from
(
5.5
)
that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Delta_{3R})}+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Delta_{3R})}+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}|+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\left\{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|+\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{3R})}\right\}+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{3R})}.

By using definitions of
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
,
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}
and
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}
, this gives

(5.9)

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|

\displaystyle+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\left\{\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{3R})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{3R})}\right\}.

To estimate
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}
on
\Omega(x_{0},3R)
, we use the representation formula to obtain that

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}(x)=\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})}dy,

where
\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)
denotes the matrix of the Green function for
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
. Note that

(5.10)

\displaystyle{}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\left\{F_{kij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}\right\}+\varepsilon\lambda\chi_{j}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\left\{a_{ij}^{\alpha\delta}(x/\varepsilon)\chi_{k}^{\delta\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}\right\}.

Then by using the representation formula of Green functions
(
4.16
)
and
(
4.42
)
,

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)\gamma}(x)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\{\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma\alpha}(x,y)\}\overline{\left[F_{kij}^{\alpha\beta}(y/\varepsilon)-a_{ij}^{\alpha\delta}(y/\varepsilon)\chi_{k}^{\delta\beta}(y/\varepsilon)\right]\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial y_{j}\partial y_{k}}}dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\varepsilon\overline{\lambda}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma\alpha}(x,y)\overline{\chi_{j}^{\alpha\beta}(y/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial y_{j}}}dy.

Since
\|F_{kij}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)},\|\chi_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C
and
p>d
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\varepsilon\lambda\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma\alpha}(x,y)\overline{\chi_{j}^{\alpha\beta}(y/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial y_{j}}}dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma\alpha}(x,y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon|\lambda|R^{2}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})},

where we have used
(
4.80
)
for the second inequality. Then

\displaystyle|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\nabla_{2}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)||\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}(y)|dy+C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon|\lambda|R^{2}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega_{4R})}\left(\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\nabla_{2}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{p^{\prime}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}+C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon|\lambda|R^{2}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}.

In view of
(
4.60
)
with
k=0
, we have
\left(\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\nabla_{2}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|^{p^{\prime}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}
and then

\displaystyle|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}(x)|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\left\{R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega_{4R})}+|\lambda|R^{2}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\}.

This, together with
(
5.9
)
, gives the proof for the case that
\Delta(x_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
. If
\Delta(x_{0},3R)=\emptyset
, we can choose
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}
and
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}
by

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})\text{ in }B\left(x_{0},3R\right)\quad\text{and}\quad w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\in H_{0}^{1}\left(B\left(x_{0},3R\right);\mathbb{C}^{m}\right),

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=0\text{ in }B\left(x_{0},3R\right)\quad\text{and}\quad w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\text{ on }\partial\left(B\left(x_{0},3R\right)\right).

By using
(
3.6
)
, it can be seen that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(2)}|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\left\{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}|+\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\left\{\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{3R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|+\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{(1)}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3R})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(B_{3R})}\right\}.

Then
(
5.9
)
is still true. Choosing
\widetilde{\Omega}=B(x_{0},3R)
and using almost the same arguments for the case that
\Delta(x_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
, we can complete the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Fix
x_{0},y_{0}\in\Omega
and
R=\frac{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}{16}>0
. For
F\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega(y_{0},R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
, let

\displaystyle u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=\int_{\Omega}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{F(y)}dy\quad\text{and}\quad u_{0,\lambda}(x)=\int_{\Omega}G_{0,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{F(y)}dy.

Then
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})=F
in
\Omega
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=u_{0,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\Omega
. For any
x\in\Omega
and
p>d
, using
(
4.60
)
with
k=0
, it follows by Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{\Omega}\nabla_{1}G_{0,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{F(y)}dy\right|\leq C\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}\left(\int_{\Omega(y_{0},R)}|\nabla_{1}G_{0,\lambda}(x,y)|^{p^{\prime}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left(\int_{\Omega(y_{0},R)}\frac{1}{|x-y|^{(d-1)p^{\prime}}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

To this end, we can obtain the
L^{\infty}
estimates of
\nabla u_{0,\lambda}
, that is,

(5.11)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

To estimate
\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}
, set
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
by

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)-u_{0,\lambda}(x)-\varepsilon\chi_{j}^{\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}=v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)+z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x),

where
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})
in
\Omega
. By using
(
2.10
)
,
(
4.6
)
and
(
5.10
)
with
p=2
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon|\lambda|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

By Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequalities, this implies that if
d\geq 3
, we have

(5.12)

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}

\displaystyle\leq CR\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2d}{d-2}}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}\leq CR\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1+\frac{d}{2}-\frac{d}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))},

where
p>d
. If
d=2
, one can use the following estimate

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}\leq C\varepsilon R\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

in place of
(
5.12
)
. Indeed, for any
2<q<\infty
, by using
(
5.10
)
, Hölder’s inequality and Sobolev embdeding theorem that
W_{0}^{1,\frac{2q}{q+2}}(\Omega)\subset L^{q}(\Omega)
, it can be got that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{1-\frac{2}{q}}\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{q}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}\leq CR^{1-\frac{2}{q}}\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{q}(\Omega)}\leq CR^{1-\frac{2}{q}}\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q+2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1-\frac{2}{q}}\left\{\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q+2}}(\Omega)}+|\lambda|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q+2}}(\Omega)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1-\frac{2}{q}}\|F\|_{L^{\frac{2q}{q+2}}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}R\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(y_{0},R))},

where for the forth and fifth inequalities, we have used Theorem
1.2
and
(
4.6
)
. Since
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(z_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega
and
z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
on
\partial\Omega
, by maximum principle
(
5.4
)
,

\displaystyle\|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}.

In view of
(
5.11
)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}+C\varepsilon R^{\frac{d}{2}}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}+\|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}+C\varepsilon R^{\frac{d}{2}}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}+C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{\frac{d}{2}}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1+\frac{d}{2}-\frac{d}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))},

where
p>d
and
d\geq 3
. Similarly, if
d=2
, we have, for
p>2
,

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}

\displaystyle\leq\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}+\|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega(x_{0},R))}+C\varepsilon R\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{2-\frac{2}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

This, together with Lemma
5.4
and
(
4.6
)
, gives

\displaystyle|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0})-u_{0,\lambda}(x_{0})|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

Then it follows by duality arguments that

(5.14)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\Omega(y_{0},R)}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y)-G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y)|^{p^{\prime}}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\text{ for any }p>d.

Finally, since
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\overline{\lambda}I)(G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x_{0},\cdot))=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\overline{\lambda}I)(G_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x_{0},\cdot))=0
in
\Omega(y_{0},R)
for any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
, we may invoke Lemma
5.4
again to conclude that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.15)

\displaystyle|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})-G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},R)}|G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x_{0},y)-G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y)|dy

\displaystyle+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1-\frac{d}{p}}\|\nabla_{2}^{2}G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}

\displaystyle+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon(1+|\lambda|R^{2})\|\nabla_{2}G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(y_{0},R))}.

Firstly for the first term of
(
5.15
)
, in view of
\eqref{ww76},
we can obtain that it is bounded by

(5.16)

C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{-k}R^{1-d}.

For the third term of
(
5.15
)
, using
(
4.60
)
, it is also bounded by
(
5.16
)
. For the second term, if
d\geq 3
, to obtain the same boundedness, we can use the
W^{2,p}
estimates for
\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I
,
(
4.8
)
, that is,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},R)}|\nabla_{2}^{2}G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y)|^{p}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{2}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega(y_{0},2R)}|G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This, together with
(
4.18
)
, completes the proof for the case that
d\geq 3
. If
d=2
, we divide the proof into two cases. If
\Delta(y_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
, then it follows directly from
(
4.50
)
and
(
5.17
)
. If
\Delta(y_{0},3R)=\emptyset
, we can obtain the same result by using the interior Lipschitz estimate for the function
\nabla_{2}G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y)
,

(5.18)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{2}^{2}G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y_{0})|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B(y_{0},2R)}|\nabla_{2}G_{0,\lambda}(x_{0},y)|^{2}dy\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

and
(
4.60
)
.
∎

Theorem 5.5

(Convergence of Green functions II)
.

For
\varepsilon>0
and
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{2,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.5
)
. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
1\leq\alpha\leq m
and
x,y\in\Omega
with
x\neq y
,

(5.19)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\{G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha\beta}(x,y)\}-\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\{\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}(x)\}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}\{G_{0,\lambda}^{\beta\gamma}(x,y)\}\right|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}|x-y|+2]}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}},

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends only on
\mu,d,m,\nu,\tau,k,\theta_{0}
and
\Omega
.

Lemma 5.6
.

For
\varepsilon>0
and
d\geq 2
,
\lambda\in\Sigma_{\theta_{0}}\cup\{0\}
with
\theta_{0}\in(0,\frac{\pi}{2})
, let
\Omega
be a bounded
C^{2,1}
domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Suppose that
A
satisfies
(
1.2
)
,
(
1.3
)
,
(
1.4
)
and
(
1.5
)
. Assume that
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H^{1}(\Omega(x_{0},4R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
u_{0,\lambda}\in C^{2,\rho}(\Omega(x_{0},4R);\mathbb{C}^{m})
for some
0<\rho<1
. If
\Delta(x_{0},4R)\neq\emptyset
, assume that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})\text{ in }\Omega(x_{0},4R)\quad\text{and}\quad u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=u_{0,\lambda}\text{ on }\Delta(x_{0},4R).

If
\Delta(x_{0},4R)=\emptyset
, assume that

\displaystyle(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})\text{ in }B(x_{0},4R).

Then for any
0<\varepsilon<r
,
1\leq\alpha\leq m
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{4R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|

(5.20)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\left\{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})R^{-1}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}R+2]\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}+\left.R^{\rho}[\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}]_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\},

where
C_{k,\theta_{0}}
depends on
\mu,d,m,k,\theta_{0},\nu,\tau,p,\rho
and
\Omega
.

Proof.

We only prove the case that
\Delta(x_{0},3R)\neq\emptyset
and the other is similar in view of the proof of Lemma
5.4
. We start by choosing a
C^{2,1}
domain
\widetilde{\Omega}
such that
\Omega(x_{0},3R)\subset\widetilde{\Omega}\subset\Omega(x_{0},4R)
. Let

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)-u_{0,\lambda}(x)-[\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\beta}(x)-P_{j}^{\beta}(x)]\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}.

Simple calculations imply that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\left\{(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})\right\}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\left\{F_{jik}^{\alpha\gamma}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}\right\}

\displaystyle+a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\beta\gamma}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\beta\gamma}-\varepsilon\chi_{k}^{\beta\gamma}(x/\varepsilon)]\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{k}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\left\{a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\beta\gamma}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\beta\gamma}]\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\lambda[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\alpha\beta}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\alpha\beta}]\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{k}}

Note that
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\Delta(x_{0},4R)
. Write
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}+z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
, where
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\in H_{0}^{1}(\widetilde{\Omega};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(v_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
. Since
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(z_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=0
in
\widetilde{\Omega}
and
z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\Delta(x_{0},3R)
, it follows from the boundary Lipschitz estimate
(
3.6
)
and
(
3.8
)
that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{R}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{3/2R}}|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{C_{\theta_{0}}}{R}\|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{3/2R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{2R}}|z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{2R}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{R}\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{2R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{R}\left\{\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}\right\},

where we have used
(
1.15
)
. This implies that

\displaystyle\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}\leq\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}+\|\nabla z_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq\frac{C_{k\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{2R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|+\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{R}\left\{R\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}\right\},

where we have used
\|v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}\leq CR\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}
since
v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=0
on
\partial\widetilde{\Omega}
. Owing to

\displaystyle\frac{\partial w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}=\frac{\partial u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}-[\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}(x)-x_{j}\delta^{\alpha\beta}]\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}},

we can obtain that for any
1\leq\alpha\leq m
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}\leq\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}+\|[\Phi_{\varepsilon}-P]\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}+C\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\Omega_{2R}}|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}|

\displaystyle+C_{k,\theta_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon R^{-1}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}+\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}+\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{2R})}\right\}.

It remains to estimate
\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
in
\Omega_{2R}
. To this end, we use the representation formula

\displaystyle v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(w_{\varepsilon,\lambda})(y)}dy,

where
\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)
is the Green function for
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I
in the
C^{2,1}
domain
\widetilde{\Omega}
. Let

\displaystyle f_{i}^{\alpha}(x)=-\varepsilon F_{jik}^{\alpha\gamma}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}+a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\beta\gamma}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\beta\gamma}]\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}.

In view of
(
5.21
)
, we can obtain

\displaystyle v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\{\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\}\overline{[f_{i}(y)-f_{i}(x)]}dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{a_{ij}(y/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}(y)-P_{k}(y)-\varepsilon\chi_{k}(y/\varepsilon)]\frac{\partial^{2}u_{0,\lambda}}{\partial y_{i}\partial y_{k}}}dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\overline{\lambda}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)\overline{[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}(y)-P_{k}(y)]\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}}{\partial y_{k}}}dy.

It follows that

(5.23)

\displaystyle|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)||f(y)-f(x)|dy

\displaystyle+C\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\nabla_{1}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)||\nabla[\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)-P(y)-\varepsilon\chi(y/\varepsilon)]|dy

\displaystyle+C|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)||\Phi_{\varepsilon}(y)-P(y)|dy.

In view of the Lipshcitz estimates of Green functions,
(
4.63
)
, we have

(5.24)

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d}}\text{ for any }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Meanwhile, simple calculations and
(
1.15
)
give the
L^{\infty}
and Hölder estimates of
f
, that is

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})},

\displaystyle|f(x)-f(y)|

\displaystyle\leq C|x-y|^{\rho}\left\{\varepsilon^{1-\rho}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}+\varepsilon[\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}]_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\}.

Choosing
k=0
in
(
5.24
)
, it can be obtained that

\displaystyle\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}}|\nabla_{1}\nabla_{2}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)||f(y)-f(x)|dy

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}\backslash B(x,\varepsilon)}\frac{dy}{|x-y|^{d}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon^{1-\rho}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}+\varepsilon[\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}]_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\}\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}\cap B(x,\varepsilon)}\frac{dy}{|x-y|^{d-\rho}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}R+2]\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}+C\varepsilon^{1+\rho}[\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}]_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\}.

Finally, using the estimates

\displaystyle|\nabla_{1}\widetilde{G}_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda||x-y|^{2})^{k}|x-y|^{d-1}}\min\left\{1,\frac{\dist(y,\partial\widetilde{\Omega})}{|x-y|}\right\}\text{ for any }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},

as well as the observation that for any
1\leq j\leq d
and
1\leq\beta\leq m
,

\displaystyle\left|\nabla\left\{\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\beta}(x)-P_{j}^{\beta}(x)-\varepsilon\chi_{j}^{\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\right\}\right|\leq C\min\left\{1,\varepsilon[\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\widetilde{\Omega})]^{-1}\right\},

we can bound the second term in the right hand side of
(
5.23
)
by

\displaystyle C_{\theta_{0}}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\left\{\varepsilon\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}\backslash B(x,\varepsilon)}\frac{dy}{|x-y|^{d}}+\int_{\widetilde{\Omega}\cap B(x,\varepsilon)}\frac{dy}{|x-y|^{d-1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}R+2]\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}.

For the third term of the right hand side of
(
5.23
)
, by rescaling and
(
4.80
)
, we can obtain that this term is bounded by

\displaystyle C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon(1+|\lambda|R^{2})R^{-1}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}.

As a result, we have proved that

\displaystyle\|\nabla v_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{3R})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon(1+|\lambda|R^{2})R^{-1}\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C_{\theta_{0}}\left\{\varepsilon\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}R+2]\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}+\varepsilon R^{\rho}[\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}]_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega_{4R})}\right\}.

This, together with
(
5.22
)
, completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

Fix
x_{0},y_{0}\in\Omega
and
R=\frac{|x_{0}-y_{0}|}{16}
. We may assume that
0<\varepsilon<r
, since the case
\varepsilon\geq r
is trivial and follows directly from the size estimates of
|\nabla_{1}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|
,
|\nabla_{2}G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x,y)|
(see
(
4.60
)
) and
(
1.15
)
. For any
1\leq\gamma\leq m
, let
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=G_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x,y_{0})
and
u_{0,\lambda}(x)=G_{0,\lambda}^{\gamma}(x,y_{0})
. Observe that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})=0
in
\Omega(x_{0},4R)
and
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=u_{0,\lambda}=0
on
\Delta(x_{0},4R)
(if
\Delta(x_{0},4R)\neq\emptyset
). By Theorem
5.1
, it can be obtained,

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}-u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega(x_{0},4R))}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon R^{1-d}.

Also, since
\Omega
is
C^{2,1}
, we have, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{d-1}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4R})}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{d}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{C^{0,\rho}(\Omega_{4R})}\leq\frac{C_{k,\theta_{0}}}{(1+|\lambda|R^{2})^{k}R^{d+\rho}}.

Here, we have used
(
4.9
)
,
(
4.10
)
,
(
4.60
)
and arguments in the proof of Theorem
5.1
. Hence, in view of
(
5.20
)
, we can complete the proof.
∎

5.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1
, Theorem
1.3
and
1.4

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

For
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}=R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon})F
and
u_{0,\lambda}=R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{0})F
, let

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)=u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}(x)-u_{0,\lambda}(x)-[\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\beta}(x)-P_{j}^{\beta}(x)]\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}.

Using the equality
(
5.21
)
and choosing
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
as the test function, we can obtain that

(5.25)

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[w_{\varepsilon,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]=\int_{\Omega}A(x/\varepsilon)\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\overline{\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}}dx-\lambda\int_{\Omega}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}dx=J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{0,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}],\quad\varepsilon>0,

where the bilinear form
J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[\cdot,\cdot]:H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})\times H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})\to\mathbb{C}
is defined by

(5.26)

\displaystyle J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u,v]

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}\varepsilon F_{jik}^{\alpha\gamma}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial^{2}u^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}\overline{\frac{\partial v^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}}dx+\lambda\int_{\Omega}[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\alpha\beta}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\alpha\beta}]\frac{\partial u^{\beta}}{\partial x_{k}}\overline{v^{\alpha}}dx

\displaystyle-\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\beta\gamma}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\beta\gamma}]\frac{\partial^{2}u^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}\partial x_{k}}\overline{\frac{\partial v^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}}dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}[\Phi_{\varepsilon,k}^{\beta\gamma}(x)-x_{k}\delta^{\beta\gamma}-\varepsilon\chi_{k}^{\beta\gamma}(x/\varepsilon)]\frac{\partial^{2}u^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{k}}\overline{v^{\alpha}}dx.

Taking
u=u_{0,\lambda}
and
v=w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
and using
(
1.15
)
, it can be inferred that

\displaystyle|J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{0,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}||\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|dx+C\varepsilon|\lambda|\int_{\Omega}|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}||w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|dx

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\Omega}|\nabla[\Phi_{\varepsilon}(x)-P(x)-\varepsilon\chi(x/\varepsilon)]||\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}||w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|dx

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\nabla[\Phi_{\varepsilon}(\cdot)-P(\cdot)-\varepsilon\chi(\cdot/\varepsilon)]w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

To estimate
|J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{0,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|
, we first claim that for any
1\leq j\leq d
and
1\leq\beta\leq m
,

(5.27)

\displaystyle\|\nabla[\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\beta}(\cdot)-P_{j}^{\beta}(\cdot)-\varepsilon\chi_{j}^{\beta}(\cdot/\varepsilon)]w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\varepsilon\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

To see
(
5.27
)
, we fix
1\leq\beta_{0}\leq m
,
1\leq j_{0}\leq d
and let

\displaystyle h_{\varepsilon}(x)=\Phi_{\varepsilon,j_{0}}^{\beta_{0}}(x)-P_{j_{0}}^{\beta_{0}}(x)-\varepsilon\chi_{j_{0}}^{\beta_{0}}(x/\varepsilon),\text{ where }x\in\Omega.

Note that
h_{\varepsilon}\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})\cap L^{\infty}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}(h_{\varepsilon})=0
in
\Omega
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mu\int_{\Omega}|\nabla h_{\varepsilon}|^{2}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Omega}a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\overline{\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{\Omega}\overline{h_{\varepsilon}^{\alpha}}a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\overline{\frac{\partial w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{i}}}w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}dx-\int_{\Omega}\overline{h_{\varepsilon}^{\alpha}}a_{ij}^{\alpha\beta}(x/\varepsilon)\frac{\partial h_{\varepsilon}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\frac{\partial w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}}{\partial x_{i}}\overline{w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\gamma}}dx,

where we have used integration by parts. Hence

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla h_{\varepsilon}|^{2}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}dx\leq C\int_{\Omega}|h_{\varepsilon}||\nabla h_{\varepsilon}||\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}||w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|dx,

where
C
depends on
d,m
and
\mu
. This directly implies the claim by noticing that
\|h_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C\varepsilon
and using the inequality

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\nabla h_{\varepsilon}|^{2}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}dx\leq\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla h_{\varepsilon}|^{2}|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}dx+C\int_{\Omega}|h_{\varepsilon}|^{2}|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}|^{2}dx,

where we have used the
(
2.26
)
. Then the claim implies that

(5.28)

\displaystyle|J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{0,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|\leq C\varepsilon|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C\varepsilon\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

In view of
(
2.9
)
,
(
2.26
)
and
(
5.25
)
, it can be easily shown that

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Cc(\lambda,\theta)}{R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|}|B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[w_{\varepsilon,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|\leq\frac{Cc(\lambda,\theta)}{R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|}|J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{0,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\varepsilon c(\lambda,\theta)}{R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|}\left\{|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\frac{C\varepsilon c(\lambda,\theta)}{R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Then it is not hard to obtain the
L^{2}
estimate of
w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}
, that is,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\frac{C\varepsilon c(\lambda,\theta)}{R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|}\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Similarly, owing to
(
2.8
)
, it can be obtained without difficulty that

\displaystyle\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Cc(\lambda,\theta)|B_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[w_{\varepsilon,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|\leq Cc(\lambda,\theta)|J_{\varepsilon,\lambda,\Omega}[u_{0,\lambda},w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}]|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon c(\lambda,\theta)\left\{|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+|\lambda|\|w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\varepsilon c(\lambda,\theta)\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

This, together with
(
2.26
)
and
(
5.29
)
, gives that

\displaystyle\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\varepsilon c(\lambda,\theta)\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{2}c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)|\lambda|\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2}c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

In view of
(
2.10
)
and
(
2.11
)
, we can estimate
\|\nabla u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
and
\|\nabla^{2}u_{0,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
. Then

\displaystyle\|\nabla w_{\varepsilon,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\varepsilon c^{2}(\lambda,\theta)\|F\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

which completes the proof of
(
1.17
)
. This, together with
(
5.29
)
, shows
(
1.16
)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

In view of the representation formula
(
4.16
)
and
(
5.1
)
, it can be seen that

\displaystyle\|R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon})-R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)\to L^{\infty}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle\|R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon})-R(\lambda,\mathcal{L}_{0})\|_{L^{1}(\Omega)\to L^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon(R_{0}^{-2}+|\lambda|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

These, together with
(
1.18
)
and the M. Riesz interpolation theorem, give
(
1.31
)
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

It follows directly from
(
5.19
)
and the arguments in Theorem 6.5.2 in
[
21
]
. We give the proof here for the sake of completeness. Let
u_{\varepsilon,\lambda},u_{0,\lambda}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon}-\lambda I)(u_{\varepsilon,\lambda})=F
and
(\mathcal{L}_{0}-\lambda I)(u_{0,\lambda})=F
with
F\in L^{p}(\Omega;\mathbb{C}^{m})
. By using
(
1.15
)
and
(
4.6
)
, we only need to show that for any
1\leq p\leq\infty
and
1\leq\alpha\leq m
,

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\varepsilon\{\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}R_{0}+2]\}^{4|\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{p}|}\|F\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}.

In view of Theorem
5.5
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\partial u_{\varepsilon,\lambda}^{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-\frac{\partial\Phi_{\varepsilon,j}^{\alpha\beta}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial u_{0,\lambda}^{\beta}}{\partial x_{j}}\right|\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\int_{\Omega}K_{\varepsilon}(x,y)|F(y)|dy,

where the kernel
K_{\varepsilon}
is defined by

\displaystyle K_{\varepsilon}(x,y)=\left\{\begin{matrix}\varepsilon|x-y|^{-d}\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}|x-y|+2]&\text{if}&|x-y|\geq\varepsilon,\\
|x-y|^{1-d}&\text{if}&|x-y|<\varepsilon.\end{matrix}\right.

Direct computations imply that

(5.30)

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\Omega}\int_{\Omega}K_{\varepsilon}(x,y)dy+\sup_{y\in\Omega}\int_{\Omega}K_{\varepsilon}(x,y)dy\leq C_{\theta_{0}}\{\ln[\varepsilon^{-1}R_{0}+2]\}^{2}.

This gives
(
1.32
)
for cases
p=1
and
\infty
. Thus, by the M. Riesz interpolation theorem, the result is a direct consequence of the case
p=2
, which is given by
(
1.17
)
.
∎
